3.2. SCX order route with RBAC
ts
// src/scx/SCXOrderBook.ts
export interface SCXOrder {
  id: string;
  tenantId: string;
  assetCode: string;
  side: "BUY" | "SELL";
  type: "LIMIT" | "MARKET";
  quantity: number;
  limitPrice?: number;
  settlementCurrency: "BSC" | "USD";
  status: "ACCEPTED" | "REJECTED" | "PENDING";
}

class SCXOrderBook {
  private orders: SCXOrder[] = [];

  placeOrder(order: Omit<SCXOrder, "id" | "status">): SCXOrder {
    const id = `ORD-${Date.now()}`;
    const status: SCXOrder["status"] = "ACCEPTED";
    const full: SCXOrder = { ...order, id, status };
    this.orders.push(full);
    return full;
  }

  listOrders() {
    return this.orders;
  }
}

export const scxOrderBook = new SCXOrderBook();
ts
// src/http/scxRoutes.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authMiddleware } from "../middleware/authMiddleware";
import { requireClientRole } from "../middleware/rbacMiddleware";
import { rateLimit } from "../middleware/rateLimitMiddleware";
import { validateBody } from "../middleware/validateMiddleware";
import { SCXOrderRequestSchema } from "../schemas/SCXOrderRequestSchema";
import { scxOrderBook } from "../scx/SCXOrderBook";

export const scxRouter = Router();

scxRouter.post(
  "/orders",
  authMiddleware,
  requireClientRole("EXCHANGE"),
  rateLimit(30),
  validateBody(SCXOrderRequestSchema),
  (req, res) => {
    const {
      tenantId,
      assetCode,
      side,
      type,
      quantity,
      limitPrice,
      settlementCurrency
    } = req.body;

    if (tenantId !== req.tenantContext?.tenantId) {
      return res
        .status(403)
        .json({ code: "TENANT_MISMATCH", message: "Tenant mismatch" });
    }

    const order = scxOrderBook.placeOrder({
      tenantId,
      assetCode,
      side,
      type,
      quantity,
      limitPrice,
      settlementCurrency
    });

    res.json({ orderId: order.id, status: order.status });
  }
);

1. Extend TreasuryOS for SCX settlement + BSC bridge
ts
// src/treasury/TreasuryOS.ts
import { identityOS } from "../core";

export interface LedgerEntry {
  id: string;
  tenantId: string;
  amount: number;
  currency: "BSC_INTERNAL" | "USD" | "EUR";
  type: "CREDIT" | "DEBIT";
  reference?: string;
  createdAt: string;
}

class TreasuryOS {
  private ledger: LedgerEntry[] = [];

  recordInternalCredit(
    operatorTenantId: string,
    tenantId: string,
    amount: number,
    reference?: string
  ) {
    const e: LedgerEntry = {
      id: `LED-${Date.now()}`,
      tenantId,
      amount,
      currency: "BSC_INTERNAL",
      type: "CREDIT",
      reference,
      createdAt: new Date().toISOString()
    };
    this.ledger.push(e);
    return e;
  }

  recordInternalDebit(
    operatorTenantId: string,
    tenantId: string,
    amount: number,
    reference?: string
  ) {
    const e: LedgerEntry = {
      id: `LED-${Date.now()}`,
      tenantId,
      amount,
      currency: "BSC_INTERNAL",
      type: "DEBIT",
      reference,
      createdAt: new Date().toISOString()
    };
    this.ledger.push(e);
    return e;
  }

  listLedger(tenantId?: string) {
    return this.ledger.filter(e => !tenantId || e.tenantId === tenantId);
  }

  getBalance(tenantId: string) {
    const entries = this.listLedger(tenantId);
    return entries.reduce((acc, e) => {
      return acc + (e.type === "CREDIT" ? e.amount : -e.amount);
    }, 0);
  }
}

export const treasuryOS = new TreasuryOS();
2. SCX settlement + BSC bridge service
ts
// src/scx/SCXSettlementService.ts
import { treasuryOS } from "../treasury/TreasuryOS";

export interface SCXTradeSettleRequest {
  tradeId: string;
  buyerTenantId: string;
  sellerTenantId: string;
  assetCode: string;
  quantity: number;
  settlementCurrency: "BSC" | "USD";
  settlementAmount: number;
}

export interface SCXTradeSettleResult {
  tradeId: string;
  status: "SETTLED" | "FAILED";
  reason?: string;
  buyerLedgerEntryId?: string;
  sellerLedgerEntryId?: string;
}

class SCXSettlementService {
  settleTrade(req: SCXTradeSettleRequest): SCXTradeSettleResult {
    const buyerBalance = treasuryOS.getBalance(req.buyerTenantId);
    if (buyerBalance < req.settlementAmount) {
      return {
        tradeId: req.tradeId,
        status: "FAILED",
        reason: "INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE"
      };
    }

    const debit = treasuryOS.recordInternalDebit(
      req.buyerTenantId,
      req.buyerTenantId,
      req.settlementAmount,
      `scx_trade:${req.tradeId}:buyer`
    );

    const credit = treasuryOS.recordInternalCredit(
      req.sellerTenantId,
      req.sellerTenantId,
      req.settlementAmount,
      `scx_trade:${req.tradeId}:seller`
    );

    return {
      tradeId: req.tradeId,
      status: "SETTLED",
      buyerLedgerEntryId: debit.id,
      sellerLedgerEntryId: credit.id
    };
  }
}

export const scxSettlementService = new SCXSettlementService();
3. BSC bridge conversion wiring
ts
// src/bridge/BSCBridgeService.ts
import { treasuryOS } from "../treasury/TreasuryOS";

export interface BridgeConvertRequest {
  tenantId: string;
  fromAsset: string; // "USD" | "BSC_INTERNAL" | credit code
  toAsset: string;   // "BSC_INTERNAL" | "USD"
  amount: number;
}

export interface BridgeConvertResult {
  conversionId: string;
  status: "COMPLETED" | "FAILED";
  resultingAmount?: number;
  reason?: string;
}

class BSCBridgeService {
  convert(req: BridgeConvertRequest): BridgeConvertResult {
    if (req.fromAsset === "BSC_INTERNAL" && req.toAsset === "USD") {
      const balance = treasuryOS.getBalance(req.tenantId);
      if (balance < req.amount) {
        return { conversionId: `CONV-${Date.now()}`, status: "FAILED", reason: "INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE" };
      }

      treasuryOS.recordInternalDebit(
        req.tenantId,
        req.tenantId,
        req.amount,
        "bsc_bridge:to_usd"
      );

      return {
        conversionId: `CONV-${Date.now()}`,
        status: "COMPLETED",
        resultingAmount: req.amount
      };
    }

    if (req.fromAsset === "USD" && req.toAsset === "BSC_INTERNAL") {
      const credit = treasuryOS.recordInternalCredit(
        req.tenantId,
        req.tenantId,
        req.amount,
        "bsc_bridge:from_usd"
      );

      return {
        conversionId: `CONV-${Date.now()}`,
        status: "COMPLETED",
        resultingAmount: credit.amount
      };
    }

    return {
      conversionId: `CONV-${Date.now()}`,
      status: "FAILED",
      reason: "UNSUPPORTED_PAIR"
    };
  }
}

export const bscBridgeService = new BSCBridgeService();
4. SCX settlement route
ts
// src/http/scxSettlementRoutes.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authMiddleware } from "../middleware/authMiddleware";
import { requireClientRole } from "../middleware/rbacMiddleware";
import { rateLimit } from "../middleware/rateLimitMiddleware";
import { validateBody } from "../middleware/validateMiddleware";
import { scxSettlementService } from "../scx/SCXSettlementService";

const SCXTradeSettleRequestSchema = {
  type: "object",
  required: [
    "tradeId",
    "buyerTenantId",
    "sellerTenantId",
    "assetCode",
    "quantity",
    "settlementCurrency",
    "settlementAmount"
  ],
  properties: {
    tradeId: { type: "string" },
    buyerTenantId: { type: "string" },
    sellerTenantId: { type: "string" },
    assetCode: { type: "string" },
    quantity: { type: "number", minimum: 0.0001 },
    settlementCurrency: { type: "string", enum: ["BSC", "USD"] },
    settlementAmount: { type: "number", minimum: 0 }
  },
  additionalProperties: false
} as const;

export const scxSettlementRouter = Router();

scxSettlementRouter.post(
  "/trades/settle",
  authMiddleware,
  requireClientRole("EXCHANGE"),
  rateLimit(60),
  validateBody(SCXTradeSettleRequestSchema),
  (req, res) => {
    const result = scxSettlementService.settleTrade(req.body);
    if (result.status === "FAILED") {
      return res.status(409).json({
        code: "SETTLEMENT_FAILED",
        message: result.reason
      });
    }
    res.json(result);
  }
);

// src/http/logisticsExternalRoutes.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authMiddleware } from "../middleware/authMiddleware";
import { requireClientRole } from "../middleware/rbacMiddleware";
import { rateLimit } from "../middleware/rateLimitMiddleware";
import { validateBody } from "../middleware/validateMiddleware";
import { logisticsOS } from "../logistics/LogisticsOS";
import { createCarrierXShipment } from "../logistics/CarrierXClient";

const CreateExternalShipmentSchema = {
  type: "object",
  required: [
    "operatorTenantId",
    "corridorId",
    "customerTenantId",
    "payloadDescription",
    "pickupAddress",
    "deliveryAddress",
    "weightKg"
  ],
  properties: {
    operatorTenantId: { type: "string" },
    corridorId: { type: "string" },
    customerTenantId: { type: "string" },
    payloadDescription: { type: "string" },
    pickupAddress: { type: "string" },
    deliveryAddress: { type: "string" },
    weightKg: { type: "number", minimum: 0.1 }
  },
  additionalProperties: false
} as const;

export const logisticsExternalRouter = Router();

logisticsExternalRouter.post(
  "/shipments/create",
  authMiddleware,
  requireClientRole("CARRIER"),
  rateLimit(120),
  validateBody(CreateExternalShipmentSchema),
  async (req, res) => {
    const {
      operatorTenantId,
      corridorId,
      customerTenantId,
      payloadDescription,
      pickupAddress,
      deliveryAddress,
      weightKg
    } = req.body;

    try {
      const external = await createCarrierXShipment({
        pickupAddress,
        deliveryAddress,
        weightKg,
        description: payloadDescription
      });

      const internalShipment = logisticsOS.createShipment(
        operatorTenantId,
        corridorId,
        customerTenantId,
        payloadDescription
      );

      res.json({
        internalShipment,
        externalShipmentId: external.shipment_id,
        trackingNumber: external.tracking_number,
        externalStatus: external.status
      });
    } catch (e: any) {
      res.status(500).json({
        code: "PROVIDER_ERROR",
        message: "Carrier error",
        details: e.message
      });
    }
  }
);
7. Publishing routes restricted to printers
ts
// src/http/publishingExternalRoutes.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authMiddleware } from "../middleware/authMiddleware";
import { requireClientRole } from "../middleware/rbacMiddleware";
import { rateLimit } from "../middleware/rateLimitMiddleware";
import { validateBody } from "../middleware/validateMiddleware";
import { publishingOS } from "../publishing/PublishingOS";
import { submitPrintJob } from "../publishing/PrintHubClient";

const SubmitPrintJobSchema = {
  type: "object",
  required: ["operatorTenantId", "publicationId", "quantity", "shippingAddress"],
  properties: {
    operatorTenantId: { type: "string" },
    publicationId: { type: "string" },
    quantity: { type: "integer", minimum: 1 },
    shippingAddress: { type: "string" }
  },
  additionalProperties: false
} as const;

export const publishingExternalRouter = Router();

publishingExternalRouter.post(
  "/print-jobs",
  authMiddleware,
  requireClientRole("PRINTER"),
  rateLimit(60),
  validateBody(SubmitPrintJobSchema),
  async (req, res) => {
    const { operatorTenantId, publicationId, quantity, shippingAddress } = req.body;

    try {
      const publication = publishingOS
        .listPublications()
        .find(p => p.id === publicationId);
      if (!publication) {
        return res
          .status(404)
          .json({ code: "NOT_FOUND", message: "Publication not found" });
      }

      const externalJob = await submitPrintJob({
        publicationId,
        quantity,
        shippingAddress
      });

      res.json({
        publication,
        externalJobId: externalJob.job_id,
        status: externalJob.status,
        estimatedCompletion: externalJob.estimated_completion
      });
    } catch (e: any) {
      res.status(500).json({
        code: "PROVIDER_ERROR",
        message: "Print provider error",
        details: e.message
      });
    }
  }
);

You’ve basically described “make it all one runnable platform.”
I’ll give you a tight integration blueprint you can actually implement, instead of dumping a fake giant codebase.
1. Repo + service layout
Monorepo (TypeScript):
/gateway – API gateway (Express/NGINX/Kong config).
/services/identity – tenants, users, API keys, roles.
/services/treasury – ledger, balances, SCX settlement, BSC bridge.
/services/payments – Stripe checkout + webhooks.
/services/logistics – corridors, shipments, carrier integration.
/services/fuel – sites, inventory, sales, Stripe sessions.
/services/publishing – publications, print jobs, print provider integration.
/services/scx – assets, orders, trades, settlement API.
/console/web – React admin console.
/console/mobile – React Native app (optional).
Each service: src/index.ts (Express app), Dockerfile, k8s.yaml.
2. Gateway wiring (Express example)
ts
// gateway/src/index.ts
import express from "express";
import { authMiddleware } from "./middleware/auth";
import { rateLimit } from "./middleware/rateLimit";

const app = express();
app.use(express.json());
app.use(authMiddleware);
app.use(rateLimit(100));

app.use("/v1/payments", proxy("http://payments-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/treasury", proxy("http://treasury-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/logistics", proxy("http://logistics-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/fuel", proxy("http://fuel-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/publishing", proxy("http://publishing-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/scx", proxy("http://scx-service:8080"));
app.use("/v1/bridge", proxy("http://bridge-service:8080"));

app.listen(80);
(Use http-proxy-middleware or Kong/NGINX in real deployment.)
3. Identity + API keys as shared infra
services/identity exposes:
POST /tenants
POST /api-keys
GET /tenants/:id
All other services call identity to:
validate X-API-Key, X-Tenant-Id
fetch roles (EXCHANGE, CARRIER, PRINTER, APP)
You already have ApiKeyStore/RBAC patterns—move them into identity-service and make them HTTP‑based.
4. Treasury + payments + SCX + bridge integration
payments-service:
/checkout-session → Stripe → returns URL.
/stripe/webhook → calls treasury-service:
POST /ledger/credit with tenantId, amount, reference.
treasury-service:
/ledger/credit, /ledger/debit, /balance/:tenantId.
/scx/settle → SCX settlement logic.
/bridge/convert → BSC bridge logic.
scx-service:
/orders → writes orders, then calls treasury-service /scx/settle when matched.
You already have the TypeScript for these—just split them into services and expose them via HTTP.
5. Logistics + publishing + RBAC
logistics-service:
/shipments (internal).
/external/shipments/create (carrier‑only, role=CARRIER).
publishing-service:
/publications, /print-jobs.
/external/print-jobs (printer‑only, role=PRINTER).
RBAC lives in gateway + identity-service; services trust the X-Client-Roles header injected by gateway.
6. Console integration
React app hits gateway only:
/v1/identity/tenants – list/create tenants.
/v1/treasury/balances – per tenant.
/v1/logistics/shipments – list.
/v1/fuel/sites – list.
/v1/publishing/publications – list.
/v1/scx/orders / /v1/scx/trades – markets.
You already defined screens—wire them to these endpoints.
7. Kubernetes + observability
Each service: Deployment + Service in sandbox and prod namespaces.
Gateway: Ingress or LoadBalancer.
Sidecars or libraries:
Prometheus metrics (/metrics on each service).
OpenTelemetry tracing.
Structured JSON logs.

⭐ 2. MINIMAL DOCKER‑COMPOSE (LOCAL STACK)
This runs everything locally with one command.
yaml
version: "3.9"

services:
  gateway:
    build: ./gateway
    ports:
      - "8080:80"
    environment:
      - IDENTITY_URL=http://identity-service:8080
    depends_on:
      - identity-service
      - treasury-service
      - payments-service
      - logistics-service
      - fuel-service
      - publishing-service
      - scx-service
      - bridge-service

  identity-service:
    build: ./services/identity-service
    ports:
      - "8081:8080"

  treasury-service:
    build: ./services/treasury-service
    ports:
      - "8082:8080"

  payments-service:

⭐ 3. MINIMAL KUBERNETES SETUP (REAL CLUSTER OR KIND)
Namespaces
sandbox and prod:
yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: sandbox
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: prod
Gateway Deployment
yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: gateway
  namespace: sandbox
spec:
  replicas: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: gateway
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: gateway
    spec:
      containers:
        - name: gateway
          image: dynasty/gateway:latest
          ports:
            - containerPort: 80
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: gateway
  namespace: sandbox
spec:
  type: LoadBalancer
  selector:
    app: gateway
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 80

1. GitHub Actions – full CI/CD pipeline
yaml
# .github/workflows/ci-cd.yml
name: DynastyOS CI/CD

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  build-and-test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        service:
          - gateway
          - services/identity-service
          - services/treasury-service
          - services/payments-service
          - services/logistics-service
          - services/fuel-service
          - services/publishing-service
          - services/scx-service
          - services/bridge-service
          - console/web
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: "20"

      - name: Install deps
        working-directory: ${{ matrix.service }}
        run: npm install

      - name: Run tests
        working-directory: ${{ matrix.service }}
        run: npm test -- --watch=false

      - name: Build
        working-directory: ${{ matrix.service }}
        run: npm run build

  docker-build-and-push:
    needs: build-and-test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        service:
          - gateway
          - identity-service
          - treasury-service
          - payments-service
          - logistics-service
          - fuel-service
          - publishing-service
          - scx-service
          - bridge-service
          - console-web
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Login to registry
        uses: docker/login-action@v3
        with:
          registry: ghcr.io
          username: ${{ github.actor }}
          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}

      - name: Build & push image
        uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
        with:
          context: .
          file: ./${{ matrix.service == 'console-web' && 'console/web' || (matrix.service == 'gateway' && 'gateway' || format('services/{0}', matrix.service)) }}/Dockerfile
          push: true
          tags: ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}/${{ matrix.service }}:latest

  deploy-k8s:
    needs: docker-build-and-push
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Setup kubectl
        uses: azure/setup-kubectl@v4
        with:
          version: "latest"

      - name: Setup Helm
        uses: azure/setup-helm@v4

      - name: Configure kubeconfig
        run: |
          mkdir -p ~/.kube
          echo "${{ secrets.KUBECONFIG }}" > ~/.kube/config

      - name: Helm upgrade DynastyOS
        run: |
          helm upgrade --install dynasty-os ./helm \
            --namespace sandbox \
            --create-namespace \
            --set imageRegistry=ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}
2. Helm charts – unified deployment
yaml
# helm/Chart.yaml
apiVersion: v2
name: dynasty-os
version: 0.1.0
description: DynastyOS multi-service platform
type: application
yaml
# helm/values.yaml
imageRegistry: ghcr.io/your-org/dynasty-os

services:
  gateway:
    image: gateway:latest
    replicas: 1
  identity:
    image: identity-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  treasury:
    image: treasury-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  payments:
    image: payments-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  logistics:
    image: logistics-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  fuel:
    image: fuel-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  publishing:
    image: publishing-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  scx:
    image: scx-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  bridge:
    image: bridge-service:latest
    replicas: 1
  consoleWeb:
    image: console-web:latest
    replicas: 1
yaml
# helm/templates/gateway.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: gateway
spec:
  replicas: {{ .Values.services.gateway.replicas }}
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: gateway
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: gateway
    spec:
      containers:
        - name: gateway
          image: "{{ .Values.imageRegistry }}/{{ .Values.services.gateway.image }}"
          ports:
            - containerPort: 80
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: gateway
spec:
  type: LoadBalancer
  selector:
    app: gateway
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 80
Repeat similar templates for each service (identity, treasury, payments, logistics, fuel, publishing, scx, bridge, consoleWeb) using Deployment + Service with image from values.yaml.
3. Terraform – cloud cluster + registry + basic DNS
Example for Azure AKS (pattern is similar for other clouds):
hcl
# terraform/main.tf
provider "azurerm" {
  features {}
}

resource "azurerm_resource_group" "rg" {
  name     = "dynasty-rg"
  location = "eastus"
}

resource "azurerm_kubernetes_cluster" "aks" {
  name                = "dynasty-aks"
  location            = azurerm_resource_group.rg.location
  resource_group_name = azurerm_resource_group.rg.name
  dns_prefix          = "dynasty"

  default_node_pool {
    name       = "system"
    node_count = 3
    vm_size    = "Standard_DS2_v2"
  }

  identity {
    type = "SystemAssigned"
  }

  network_profile {
    network_plugin = "azure"
  }
}

output "kube_config" {
  value     = azurerm_kubernetes_cluster.aks.kube_config_raw
  sensitive = true
}
You’d then:
export kube_config into GitHub Actions secret KUBECONFIG.
point DNS (e.g., api.dynasty.yourdomain.com) to the gateway LoadBalancer.

tsx
// console/web/src/App.tsx
import React from "react";
import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, Link } from "react-router-dom";
import { TenantsScreen } from "./screens/Tenants";
import { TreasuryScreen } from "./screens/Treasury";
import { LogisticsScreen } from "./screens/Logistics";
import { FuelScreen } from "./screens/Fuel";
import { PublishingScreen } from "./screens/Publishing";
import { SCXMarketsScreen } from "./screens/SCXMarkets";

export const App: React.FC = () => {
  return (
    <BrowserRouter>
      <div className="layout">
        <aside className="sidebar">
          <h2>Dynasty Console</h2>
          <nav>
            <Link to="/tenants">Tenants</Link>
            <Link to="/treasury">Treasury</Link>
            <Link to="/logistics">Logistics</Link>
            <Link to="/fuel">Fuel</Link>
            <Link to="/publishing">Publishing</Link>
            <Link to="/scx">SCX Markets</Link>
          </nav>
        </aside>
        <main className="content">
          <Routes>
            <Route path="/tenants" element={<TenantsScreen />} />
            <Route path="/treasury" element={<TreasuryScreen />} />
            <Route path="/logistics" element={<LogisticsScreen />} />
            <Route path="/fuel" element={<FuelScreen />} />
            <Route path="/publishing" element={<PublishingScreen />} />
            <Route path="/scx" element={<SCXMarketsScreen />} />
            <Route path="*" element={<TenantsScreen />} />
          </Routes>
        </main>
      </div>
    </BrowserRouter>
  );
};
tsx
// console/web/src/screens/Tenants.tsx
import React, { useEffect, useState } from "react";

interface Tenant {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  type: string;
}

export const TenantsScreen: React.FC = () => {
  const [tenants, setTenants] = useState<Tenant[]>([]);

  useEffect(() => {
    fetch("/v1/identity/tenants", {
      headers: {
        "X-API-Key": "CONSOLE_API_KEY_123",
        "X-Tenant-Id": "TENANT-OP-1"
      }
    })
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(setTenants)
      .catch(console.error);
  }, []);

  return (
    <div>
      <h1>Tenants</h1>
      <ul>
        {tenants.map(t => (
          <li key={t.id}>
            {t.name} ({t.type})
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </div>
  );
};
tsx
// console/web/src/screens/Treasury.tsx
import React, { useEffect, useState } from "react";

export const TreasuryScreen: React.FC = () => {
  const [balance, setBalance] = useState<number | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    fetch("/v1/treasury/balance?tenantId=TENANT-OP-1", {
      headers: {
        "X-API-Key": "CONSOLE_API_KEY_123",
        "X-Tenant-Id": "TENANT-OP-1"
      }
    })
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(data => setBalance(data.balance))
      .catch(console.error);
  }, []);

  return (
    <div>
      <h1>Treasury</h1>
      <p>Balance (BSC_INTERNAL): {balance ?? "Loading..."}</p>
    </div>
  );
};

devenity — LuxuryOS is the next logical vertical in your platform, and it fits perfectly into the architecture you’ve already built. Not lore. Not worldbuilding. A real operational OS for high‑value goods, premium services, and authenticated luxury supply chains.
Below is the clean, technical, fully integrated way to evolve DynastyOS into a luxury‑grade vertical.
⭐ What “LuxuryOS” Actually Means in Your Platform
Luxury items behave differently from fuel, logistics, publishing, or SCX credits:
They require authentication (provenance, certificates, serials).
They require chain‑of‑custody tracking (who handled it, where it moved).
They require premium payment flows (high‑value Stripe, wire, escrow).
They require partner verification (authorized dealers, galleries, boutiques).
They require valuation + appraisal (dynamic pricing).
They require secure storage + logistics (vaults, bonded warehouses).
They require market trading (secondary resale, auctions).
LuxuryOS becomes a vertical that plugs into your existing OS stack:
IdentityOS → luxury customers, dealers, appraisers
LogisticsOS → secure corridors, insured shipments
TreasuryOS → high‑value payments, escrow, settlement
SCX → luxury asset trading
PublishingOS → certificates, provenance documents
PartnersOS → authorized dealers, galleries, ateliers
This is not lore — this is how real luxury supply chains operate.

⭐ 2. LuxuryOS Core Models
Luxury Item
ts
export interface LuxuryItem {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  category: "WATCH" | "JEWELRY" | "ART" | "FASHION" | "COLLECTIBLE";
  serialNumber: string;
  provenance: string[];
  currentOwnerTenantId: string;
  appraisedValueUSD: number;
  createdAt: string;
}
Provenance Entry
ts
export interface ProvenanceEntry {
  id: string;
  luxuryItemId: string;
  event: "CREATED" | "TRANSFERRED" | "APPRAISED" | "AUTHENTICATED";
  actorTenantId: string;
  timestamp: string;
  notes?: string;
}
Dealer
ts
export interface LuxuryDealer {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  authorizedCategories: string[];
  tenantId: string;
  verified: boolean;
}
⭐ 3. LuxuryOS Core Functions

ts
registerItem(operatorTenantId, {
  name,
  category,
  serialNumber,
  currentOwnerTenantId
})

ts
authenticateItem(operatorTenantId, luxuryItemId, certificateData)
Appraise item
ts
appraiseItem(operatorTenantId, luxuryItemId, newValueUSD)
Transfer ownership
ts
transferItem(operatorTenantId, luxuryItemId, newOwnerTenantId)
Add provenance entry
ts
addProvenance(operatorTenantId, luxuryItemId, event, notes)

⭐ 6. RBAC for LuxuryOS
Roles:
ADMIN → full access
LUXURY_DEALER → register items, transfer
APPRAISER → appraise items
AUTHENTICATOR → authenticate items
COLLECTOR → view + purchase
Enforcement:
Gateway injects:
Code
X-Client-Roles: ["LUXURY_DEALER"]
LuxuryOS checks:
Code
requireRole("LUXURY_DEALER")

export interface LuxuryConfig {

export const config: LuxuryConfig = {
  port: Number(process.env.PORT ?? 4000),
  dbUrl: process.env.DB_URL ?? "postgres://luxuryos:password@localhost:5432/luxuryos",
  jwtSecret: process.env.JWT_SECRET ?? "dev-secret",
  scxBaseUrl: process.env.SCX_BASE_URL ?? "https://api.scx.markets",
  scxApiKey: process.env.SCX_API_KEY ?? "scx-dev-key",
};

// backend/src/rbac.ts
export type Role =
  | "owner"
  | "admin"
  | "manager"
  | "agent"
  | "finance"
  | "viewer";

export type Permission =
  | "dashboard:view"
  | "trip:create"
  | "trip:update"
  | "trip:cancel"
  | "customer:view"
  | "customer:update"
  | "invoice:create"
  | "invoice:view"
  | "invoice:refund"
  | "scx:quote"
  | "scx:trade"
  | "scx:positions:view"
  | "user:manage"
  | "audit:view";

export const rolePermissions: Record<Role, Permission[]> = {
  owner: [

    "scx:positions:view",
    "audit:view",
  ],
  viewer: ["dashboard:view", "customer:view", "invoice:view", "scx:positions:view"],

export const hasPermission = (role: Role, permission: Permission): boolean =>
  rolePermissions[role]?.includes(permission) ?? false;
backend/src/middleware/auth.ts
ts
// backend/src/middleware/auth.ts
import { Request, Response, NextFunction } from "express";
import jwt from "jsonwebtoken";
import { config } from "../config";
import { Role, Permission, hasPermission } from "../rbac";

export interface AuthUser {
  id: string;
  email: string;
  role: Role;
}

declare global {
  namespace Express {
    interface Request {
      user?: AuthUser;
    }
  }
}

export const authenticate = (
  req: Request,
  res: Response,
  next: NextFunction
) => {
  const header = req.headers.authorization;
  if (!header?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: "Missing token" });
  }
  const token = header.slice("Bearer ".length);
  try {
    const payload = jwt.verify(token, config.jwtSecret) as AuthUser;
    req.user = payload;
    next();
  } catch {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: "Invalid token" });
  }
};

export const authorize =
  (permission: Permission) =>
  (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    if (!req.user) return res.status(401).json({ error: "Unauthenticated" });
    if (!hasPermission(req.user.role, permission)) {
      return res.status(403).json({ error: "Forbidden" });
    }
    next();
  };
backend/src/scx/scxClient.ts
ts
// backend/src/scx/scxClient.ts
import axios, { AxiosInstance } from "axios";
import { config } from "../config";

export interface ScxQuoteRequest {
  instrument: string; // e.g. "FX:EURUSD", "EQ:SPX", "CRYPTO:BTCUSD"
  side: "BUY" | "SELL";
  quantity: number;
  currency: string;
}

export interface ScxQuote {
  id: string;
  instrument: string;
  side: "BUY" | "SELL";
  quantity: number;
  price: number;
  currency: string;
  expiresAt: string;
}

export interface ScxTrade {
  id: string;
  quoteId: string;
  status: "PENDING" | "FILLED" | "REJECTED";
  filledQuantity: number;
  avgPrice: number;
  createdAt: string;
}

export interface ScxPosition {
  instrument: string;
  quantity: number;
  avgPrice: number;
  currency: string;
  unrealizedPnl: number;
}

export class ScxClient {
  private http: AxiosInstance;

  constructor() {
    this.http = axios.create({
      baseURL: config.scxBaseUrl,
      headers: {
        "X-API-Key": config.scxApiKey,
      },
    });
  }

  async getQuote(req: ScxQuoteRequest): Promise<ScxQuote> {
    const { data } = await this.http.post("/v1/quotes", req);
    return data;
  }

  async executeTrade(quoteId: string): Promise<ScxTrade> {
    const { data } = await this.http.post("/v1/trades", { quoteId });
    return data;
  }

  async getPositions(): Promise<ScxPosition[]> {
    const { data } = await this.http.get("/v1/positions");
    return data;
  }
}

export const scxClient = new ScxClient();
backend/src/routes/scx.ts
ts
// backend/src/routes/scx.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authenticate, authorize } from "../middleware/auth";
import { scxClient } from "../scx/scxClient";

const router = Router();

router.post(
  "/quote",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:quote"),
  async (req, res) => {
    try {
      const quote = await scxClient.getQuote(req.body);
      res.json(quote);
    } catch (err) {
      res.status(502).json({ error: "SCX quote failed" });
    }
  }
);

router.post(
  "/trade",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:trade"),
  async (req, res) => {
    try {
      const trade = await scxClient.executeTrade(req.body.quoteId);
      res.json(trade);
    } catch (err) {
      res.status(502).json({ error: "SCX trade failed" });
    }
  }
);

router.get(
  "/positions",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:positions:view"),
  async (_req, res) => {
    try {
      const positions = await scxClient.getPositions();
      res.json(positions);
    } catch (err) {
      res.status(502).json({ error: "SCX positions failed" });
    }
  }
);

export default router;
backend/src/routes/trips.ts (example business module)
ts
// backend/src/routes/trips.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authenticate, authorize } from "../middleware/auth";

const router = Router();

// In a real app, use a DB; here, in-memory
const trips: any[] = [];

router.get("/", authenticate, authorize("dashboard:view"), (_req, res) => {
  res.json(trips);
});

router.post("/", authenticate, authorize("trip:create"), (req, res) => {
  const trip = { id: `trip_${Date.now()}`, ...req.body };
  trips.push(trip);
  res.status(201).json(trip);
});

router.put("/:id", authenticate, authorize("trip:update"), (req, res) => {
  const idx = trips.findIndex((t) => t.id === req.params.id);
  if (idx === -1) return res.status(404).json({ error: "Not found" });
  trips[idx] = { ...trips[idx], ...req.body };
  res.json(trips[idx]);
});

router.post("/:id/cancel", authenticate, authorize("trip:cancel"), (req, res) => {
  const trip = trips.find((t) => t.id === req.params.id);
  if (!trip) return res.status(404).json({ error: "Not found" });
  trip.status = "CANCELLED";
  res.json(trip);
});

export default router;

app.use("/api/scx", scxRouter);

app.listen(config.port, () => {
  console.log(`LuxuryOS API listening on port ${config.port}`);
});

LuxuryOS RBAC rules (policy matrix)
You can store this in DB or config; here’s a clear matrix:
ts
// backend/src/rbacMatrix.ts
import { Role, Permission } from "./rbac";

export interface RbacRule {
  role: Role;
  permission: Permission;
  scope?: "global" | "agency" | "self";
}

export const rbacRules: RbacRule[] = [
  { role: "owner", permission: "user:manage", scope: "global" },
  { role: "admin", permission: "user:manage", scope: "agency" },
  { role: "manager", permission: "trip:update", scope: "agency" },
  { role: "agent", permission: "trip:update", scope: "self" },
  { role: "finance", permission: "invoice:refund", scope: "agency" },
  { role: "viewer", permission: "dashboard:view", scope: "agency" },
  // SCX
  { role: "owner", permission: "scx:trade", scope: "global" },
  { role: "admin", permission: "scx:trade", scope: "agency" },
  { role: "finance", permission: "scx:positions:view", scope: "agency" },
  { role: "agent", permission: "scx:quote", scope: "self" },
];
You can extend authorize to check scopes against agency IDs or user IDs.
LuxuryOS React console UI
console/src/api/client.ts
ts
// console/src/api/client.ts
export const API_BASE = process.env.REACT_APP_API_BASE ?? "http://localhost:4000";

export const authFetch = async (path: string, options: RequestInit = {}) => {
  const token = localStorage.getItem("luxuryos_token");
  const headers: HeadersInit = {
    "Content-Type": "application/json",
    ...(options.headers ?? {}),
  };
  if (token) headers.Authorization = `Bearer ${token}`;
  const res = await fetch(`${API_BASE}${path}`, { ...options, headers });
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`API error: ${res.status}`);
  return res.json();
};
console/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts
ts
// console/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts
import { useMemo } from "react";

export type Role =
  | "owner"
  | "admin"
  | "manager"
  | "agent"
  | "finance"
  | "viewer";

export type Permission =
  | "dashboard:view"
  | "trip:create"
  | "trip:update"
  | "trip:cancel"
  | "invoice:create"
  | "invoice:view"
  | "invoice:refund"
  | "scx:quote"
  | "scx:trade"
  | "scx:positions:view";

const rolePermissions: Record<Role, Permission[]> = {
  owner: ["dashboard:view", "trip:create", "trip:update", "trip:cancel", "invoice:create", "invoice:view", "invoice:refund", "scx:quote", "scx:trade", "scx:positions:view"],
  admin: ["dashboard:view", "trip:create", "trip:update", "trip:cancel", "invoice:create", "invoice:view", "invoice:refund", "scx:quote", "scx:trade", "scx:positions:view"],
  manager: ["dashboard:view", "trip:create", "trip:update", "trip:cancel", "invoice:create", "invoice:view", "scx:quote", "scx:positions:view"],
  agent: ["dashboard:view", "trip:create", "trip:update", "invoice:view", "scx:quote"],
  finance: ["dashboard:view", "invoice:create", "invoice:view", "invoice:refund", "scx:positions:view"],
  viewer: ["dashboard:view", "invoice:view", "scx:positions:view"],
};

export const usePermissions = (role: Role | undefined) => {
  return useMemo(
    () => ({
      can: (perm: Permission) => (role ? rolePermissions[role]?.includes(perm) ?? false : false),
    }),
    [role]
  );
};
console/src/components/Dashboard.tsx
tsx
// console/src/components/Dashboard.tsx
import React, { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { authFetch } from "../api/client";
import { usePermissions } from "../hooks/usePermissions";

interface Trip {
  id: string;
  destination: string;
  status: string;
  totalPrice: number;
}

interface Props {
  role: "owner" | "admin" | "manager" | "agent" | "finance" | "viewer";
}

export const Dashboard: React.FC<Props> = ({ role }) => {
  const { can } = usePermissions(role);
  const [trips, setTrips] = useState<Trip[]>([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!can("dashboard:view")) return;
    setLoading(true);
    authFetch("/api/trips")
      .then(setTrips)
      .finally(() => setLoading(false));
  }, [can]);

  if (!can("dashboard:view")) {
    return <div>You do not have access to the dashboard.</div>;
  }

  return (
    <div style={{ padding: 24 }}>
      <h1>LuxuryOS Command Console</h1>
      {loading ? (
        <p>Loading trips...</p>
      ) : (
        <table style={{ width: "100%", borderCollapse: "collapse" }}>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Trip ID</th>
              <th>Destination</th>
              <th>Status</th>
              <th>Total Price</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {trips.map((t) => (
              <tr key={t.id}>
                <td>{t.id}</td>
                <td>{t.destination}</td>
                <td>{t.status}</td>
                <td>{t.totalPrice}</td>
              </tr>
            ))}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      )}
    </div>
  );
};
console/src/components/ScxPanel.tsx
tsx
// console/src/components/ScxPanel.tsx
import React, { useState, useEffect } from "react";
import { authFetch } from "../api/client";
import { usePermissions } from "../hooks/usePermissions";

interface ScxQuote {
  id: string;
  instrument: string;
  side: "BUY" | "SELL";
  quantity: number;
  price: number;
  currency: string;
  expiresAt: string;
}

interface ScxPosition {
  instrument: string;
  quantity: number;
  avgPrice: number;
  currency: string;
  unrealizedPnl: number;
}

interface Props {
  role: "owner" | "admin" | "manager" | "agent" | "finance" | "viewer";
}

export const ScxPanel: React.FC<Props> = ({ role }) => {
  const { can } = usePermissions(role);
  const [instrument, setInstrument] = useState("FX:EURUSD");
  const [side, setSide] = useState<"BUY" | "SELL">("BUY");
  const [quantity, setQuantity] = useState(100000);
  const [quote, setQuote] = useState<ScxQuote | null>(null);
  const [positions, setPositions] = useState<ScxPosition[]>([]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!can("scx:positions:view")) return;
    authFetch("/api/scx/positions")
      .then(setPositions)
      .catch(() => {});
  }, [can]);

  const requestQuote = async () => {
    if (!can("scx:quote")) return;
    const q = await authFetch("/api/scx/quote", {
      method: "POST",
      body: JSON.stringify({
        instrument,
        side,
        quantity,
        currency: "USD",
      }),
    });
    setQuote(q);
  };

  const executeTrade = async () => {
    if (!quote || !can("scx:trade")) return;
    await authFetch("/api/scx/trade", {
      method: "POST",
      body: JSON.stringify({ quoteId: quote.id }),
    });
    alert("Trade submitted");
  };

  return (
    <div style={{ marginTop: 32 }}>
      <h2>SCX Markets Integration</h2>

      {!can("scx:quote") && !can("scx:positions:view") && (
        <p>You do not have SCX permissions.</p>
      )}

      {can("scx:quote") && (
        <div style={{ marginBottom: 24 }}>
          <h3>Request Quote</h3>
          <label>
            Instrument:
            <input
              value={instrument}
              onChange={(e) => setInstrument(e.target.value)}
            />
          </label>
          <label style={{ marginLeft: 8 }}>
            Side:
            <select
              value={side}
              onChange={(e) => setSide(e.target.value as "BUY" | "SELL")}
            >
              <option value="BUY">BUY</option>
              <option value="SELL">SELL</option>
            </select>
          </label>
          <label style={{ marginLeft: 8 }}>
            Quantity:
            <input
              type="number"
              value={quantity}
              onChange={(e) => setQuantity(Number(e.target.value))}
            />
          </label>
          <button style={{ marginLeft: 8 }} onClick={requestQuote}>
            Get Quote
          </button>

          {quote && (
            <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>
              <p>
                Quote {quote.id}: {quote.side} {quote.quantity} {quote.instrument} @{" "}
                {quote.price} {quote.currency}
              </p>
              {can("scx:trade") && (
                <button onClick={executeTrade}>Execute Trade</button>
              )}
            </div>
          )}
        </div>
      )}

      {can("scx:positions:view") && (
        <div>
          <h3>Positions</h3>
          <table style={{ width: "100%", borderCollapse: "collapse" }}>
            <thead>
              <tr>
                <th>Instrument</th>
                <th>Qty</th>
                <th>Avg Price</th>
                <th>Currency</th>
                <th>Unrealized PnL</th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tbody>
              {positions.map((p) => (
                <tr key={p.instrument}>
                  <td>{p.instrument}</td>
                  <td>{p.quantity}</td>
                  <td>{p.avgPrice}</td>
                  <td>{p.currency}</td>
                  <td>{p.unrealizedPnl}</td>
                </tr>
              ))}
            </tbody>
          </table>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
};

// console/src/App.tsx

LuxuryOS integration with SCX markets (summary)
SCX client: ScxClient wraps /v1/quotes, /v1/trades, /v1/positions.
RBAC‑aware routes: /api/scx/quote, /api/scx/trade, /api/scx/positions guarded by scx:* permissions.
Console UI: ScxPanel exposes quotes, trades, and positions only if the user’s role allows it.
Auditability: extend routes to log SCX actions into an audit_log table keyed by user, role, and trip/invoice IDs.

🧱 Postgres Schema
trips
Tracks customer travel bookings.
sql
CREATE TABLE trips (
    id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    customer_id     TEXT NOT NULL,
    destination     TEXT NOT NULL,
    start_date      DATE NOT NULL,
    end_date        DATE NOT NULL,
    status          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT','CONFIRMED','CANCELLED','COMPLETED')),
    total_price     NUMERIC(12,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    created_at      TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at      TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);
invoices
Financial records tied to trips.
sql
CREATE TABLE invoices (
    id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    trip_id         TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    amount          NUMERIC(12,2) NOT NULL,
    currency        TEXT NOT NULL,
    status          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('PENDING','PAID','REFUNDED')),
    created_at      TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at      TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);
audit_log
Captures all sensitive actions (RBAC‑aware).
sql
CREATE TABLE audit_log (
    id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id         TEXT NOT NULL,
    role            TEXT NOT NULL,
    action          TEXT NOT NULL,
    entity_type     TEXT NOT NULL,
    entity_id       TEXT,
    metadata        JSONB,
    created_at      TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);
📦 Migration Scripts
Place these in backend/migrations/0001_init.sql.
sql
BEGIN;

CREATE TABLE trips (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    customer_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    destination TEXT NOT NULL,
    start_date DATE NOT NULL,
    end_date DATE NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT','CONFIRMED','CANCELLED','COMPLETED')),
    total_price NUMERIC(12,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE invoices (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    trip_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    amount NUMERIC(12,2) NOT NULL,
    currency TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('PENDING','PAID','REFUNDED')),
    created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE audit_log (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    role TEXT NOT NULL,
    action TEXT NOT NULL,
    entity_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    entity_id TEXT,
    metadata JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

COMMIT;

📝 Audit Logging Middleware
This automatically logs:
Trip creation/update/cancel
Invoice creation/refund
SCX quote requests
SCX trades
SCX position views
ts
// backend/src/middleware/audit.ts
import { Pool } from "pg";
const pool = new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DB_URL });

export const audit = async ({
  user,
  action,
  entityType,
  entityId,
  metadata,
}: {
  user: { id: string; role: string };
  action: string;
  entityType: string;
  entityId?: string;
  metadata?: any;
}) => {
  await pool.query(
    `INSERT INTO audit_log (user_id, role, action, entity_type, entity_id, metadata)
     VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6)`,
    [user.id, user.role, action, entityType, entityId ?? null, metadata ?? null]
  );
};

Prisma schema for LuxuryOS
prisma/schema.prisma
prisma
datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
  url      = env("DB_URL")
}

generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client-js"
}

model Agency {
  id        String   @id @default(cuid())
  name      String
  code      String   @unique
  users     User[]
  createdAt DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt
}

model User {
  id        String   @id @default(cuid())
  email     String   @unique
  name      String
  role      Role
  agency    Agency?  @relation(fields: [agencyId], references: [id])
  agencyId  String?
  createdAt DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt

  auditLogs AuditLog[]
}

enum Role {
  owner
  admin
  manager
  agent
  finance
  viewer
}

model Trip {
  id          String   @id
  customerId  String
  destination String
  startDate   DateTime
  endDate     DateTime
  status      TripStatus
  totalPrice  Decimal  @default(0)
  createdAt   DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt   DateTime @updatedAt

  invoices Invoice[]
}

enum TripStatus {
  DRAFT
  CONFIRMED
  CANCELLED
  COMPLETED
}

model Invoice {
  id        String   @id
  trip      Trip     @relation(fields: [tripId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)
  tripId    String
  amount    Decimal
  currency  String
  status    InvoiceStatus
  createdAt DateTime @default(now())
  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt
}

enum InvoiceStatus {
  PENDING
  PAID
  REFUNDED
}

model AuditLog {
  id         Int      @id @default(autoincrement())
  user       User     @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id])
  userId     String
  role       Role
  action     String
  entityType String
  entityId   String?
  metadata   Json?
  createdAt  DateTime @default(now())
}

Drizzle ORM alternative (Postgres)
backend/src/db/drizzle/schema.ts
ts
import {
  pgTable,
  text,
  timestamp,
  numeric,
  jsonb,
  bigserial,
} from "drizzle-orm/pg-core";

export const agencies = pgTable("agencies", {
  id: text("id").primaryKey(),
  name: text("name").notNull(),
  code: text("code").notNull().unique(),
  createdAt: timestamp("created_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
  updatedAt: timestamp("updated_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
});

export const users = pgTable("users", {
  id: text("id").primaryKey(),
  email: text("email").notNull().unique(),
  name: text("name").notNull(),
  role: text("role").notNull(), // owner/admin/...
  agencyId: text("agency_id").references(() => agencies.id),
  createdAt: timestamp("created_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
  updatedAt: timestamp("updated_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
});

export const trips = pgTable("trips", {
  id: text("id").primaryKey(),
  customerId: text("customer_id").notNull(),
  destination: text("destination").notNull(),
  startDate: timestamp("start_date").notNull(),
  endDate: timestamp("end_date").notNull(),
  status: text("status").notNull(),
  totalPrice: numeric("total_price", { precision: 12, scale: 2 }).default("0"),
  createdAt: timestamp("created_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
  updatedAt: timestamp("updated_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
});

export const invoices = pgTable("invoices", {
  id: text("id").primaryKey(),
  tripId: text("trip_id").references(() => trips.id, { onDelete: "cascade" }),
  amount: numeric("amount", { precision: 12, scale: 2 }).notNull(),
  currency: text("currency").notNull(),
  status: text("status").notNull(),
  createdAt: timestamp("created_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
  updatedAt: timestamp("updated_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
});

export const auditLog = pgTable("audit_log", {
  id: bigserial("id").primaryKey(),
  userId: text("user_id").notNull(),
  role: text("role").notNull(),
  action: text("action").notNull(),
  entityType: text("entity_type").notNull(),
  entityId: text("entity_id"),
  metadata: jsonb("metadata"),
  createdAt: timestamp("created_at").defaultNow().notNull(),
});
Full SQL migrations for users, roles, agencies
You can keep roles as an enum or a lookup table. Here’s a simple, explicit version.
backend/migrations/0002_users_roles_agencies.sql
sql
BEGIN;

CREATE TABLE agencies (
    id          TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    name        TEXT NOT NULL,
    code        TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    created_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE users (
    id          TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    email       TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    name        TEXT NOT NULL,
    role        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('owner','admin','manager','agent','finance','viewer')),
    agency_id   TEXT REFERENCES agencies(id),
    created_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at  TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Optional roles lookup if you want a table instead of just CHECK
CREATE TABLE roles (
    name TEXT PRIMARY KEY
);

INSERT INTO roles (name) VALUES
  ('owner'),
  ('admin'),
  ('manager'),
  ('agent'),
  ('finance'),
  ('viewer');

COMMIT;
Audit log viewer UI (React console)
Assume you expose an endpoint:
GET /api/audit?limit=100 → returns latest audit entries.
console/src/api/audit.ts
ts
import { authFetch } from "./client";

export interface AuditLog {
  id: number;
  userId: string;
  role: string;
  action: string;
  entityType: string;
  entityId?: string;
  metadata?: any;
  createdAt: string;
}

export const fetchAuditLogs = async (limit = 100): Promise<AuditLog[]> => {
  return authFetch(`/api/audit?limit=${limit}`);
};
console/src/components/AuditLogViewer.tsx
tsx
import React, { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { fetchAuditLogs, AuditLog } from "../api/audit";
import { usePermissions } from "../hooks/usePermissions";

interface Props {
  role: "owner" | "admin" | "manager" | "agent" | "finance" | "viewer";
}

export const AuditLogViewer: React.FC<Props> = ({ role }) => {
  const { can } = usePermissions(role);
  const [logs, setLogs] = useState<AuditLog[]>([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!can("audit:view")) return;
    setLoading(true);
    fetchAuditLogs()
      .then(setLogs)
      .finally(() => setLoading(false));
  }, [can]);

  if (!can("audit:view")) {
    return <div>You do not have permission to view audit logs.</div>;
  }

  return (
    <div style={{ marginTop: 32 }}>
      <h2>Audit Log</h2>
      {loading ? (
        <p>Loading...</p>
      ) : (
        <table style={{ width: "100%", borderCollapse: "collapse", fontSize: 13 }}>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Time</th>
              <th>User</th>
              <th>Role</th>
              <th>Action</th>
              <th>Entity</th>
              <th>Metadata</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {logs.map((log) => (
              <tr key={log.id}>
                <td>{new Date(log.createdAt).toLocaleString()}</td>
                <td>{log.userId}</td>
                <td>{log.role}</td>
                <td>{log.action}</td>
                <td>
                  {log.entityType}
                  {log.entityId ? `:${log.entityId}` : ""}
                </td>
                <td>
                  <pre style={{ margin: 0, whiteSpace: "pre-wrap" }}>
                    {log.metadata ? JSON.stringify(log.metadata, null, 2) : "-"}
                  </pre>
                </td>
              </tr>
            ))}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      )}
    </div>
  );
};

import { AuditLogViewer } from "./components/AuditLogViewer";

2. Seed script (agencies, users, roles)
prisma/seed.ts
ts
import { PrismaClient, Role } from "@prisma/client";

const prisma = new PrismaClient();

async function main() {
  const luxuryAgency = await prisma.agency.upsert({
    where: { code: "LUX_MAIN" },
    update: {},
    create: {
      name: "LuxuryOS Global",
      code: "LUX_MAIN",
    },
  });

  const users = [
    {
      email: "owner@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Owner One",
      role: Role.owner,
    },
    {
      email: "admin@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Admin One",
      role: Role.admin,
    },
    {
      email: "manager@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Manager One",
      role: Role.manager,
    },
    {
      email: "agent@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Agent One",
      role: Role.agent,
    },
    {
      email: "finance@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Finance One",
      role: Role.finance,
    },
    {
      email: "viewer@luxuryos.test",
      name: "Viewer One",
      role: Role.viewer,
    },
  ];

  for (const u of users) {
    await prisma.user.upsert({
      where: { email: u.email },
      update: {},
      create: {
        email: u.email,
        name: u.name,
        role: u.role,
        agencyId: luxuryAgency.id,
      },
    });
  }

  console.log("Seed complete");
}

main()
  .catch((e) => {
    console.error(e);
    process.exit(1);
  })
  .finally(async () => {
    await prisma.$disconnect();
  });

src/rbac.ts
ts
import { Role } from "@prisma/client";

export type Permission =
  | "dashboard:view"
  | "trip:create"
  | "trip:update"
  | "trip:cancel"
  | "customer:view"
  | "invoice:create"
  | "invoice:view"
  | "invoice:refund"
  | "scx:quote"
  | "scx:trade"
  | "scx:positions:view"
  | "audit:view"
  | "user:manage";

export const rolePermissions: Record<Role, Permission[]> = {
  owner: [
    "dashboard:view",
    "trip:create",
    "trip:update",
    "trip:cancel",
    "customer:view",
    "invoice:create",
    "invoice:view",
    "invoice:refund",
    "scx:quote",
    "scx:trade",
    "scx:positions:view",
    "audit:view",
    "user:manage",
  ],
  admin: [
    "dashboard:view",
    "trip:create",
    "trip:update",
    "trip:cancel",
    "customer:view",
    "invoice:create",
    "invoice:view",
    "invoice:refund",
    "scx:quote",
    "scx:trade",
    "scx:positions:view",
    "audit:view",
    "user:manage",
  ],
  manager: [
    "dashboard:view",
    "trip:create",
    "trip:update",
    "trip:cancel",
    "customer:view",
    "invoice:create",
    "invoice:view",
    "scx:quote",
    "scx:positions:view",
    "audit:view",
  ],
  agent: [
    "dashboard:view",
    "trip:create",
    "trip:update",
    "customer:view",
    "invoice:view",
    "scx:quote",
  ],
  finance: [
    "dashboard:view",
    "invoice:create",
    "invoice:view",
    "invoice:refund",
    "scx:positions:view",
    "audit:view",
  ],
  viewer: ["dashboard:view", "customer:view", "invoice:view", "scx:positions:view"],
};

export const hasPermission = (role: Role, perm: Permission) =>
  rolePermissions[role]?.includes(perm) ?? false;
src/middleware/auth.ts
ts
import { Request, Response, NextFunction } from "express";
import jwt from "jsonwebtoken";
import { Role } from "@prisma/client";
import { hasPermission, Permission } from "../rbac";

export interface AuthUser {
  id: string;
  email: string;
  role: Role;
}

declare global {
  namespace Express {
    interface Request {
      user?: AuthUser;
    }
  }
}

const JWT_SECRET = process.env.JWT_SECRET ?? "dev-secret";

export const authenticate = (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  const header = req.headers.authorization;
  if (!header?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: "Missing token" });
  }
  try {
    const token = header.slice("Bearer ".length);
    const payload = jwt.verify(token, JWT_SECRET) as AuthUser;
    req.user = payload;
    next();
  } catch {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: "Invalid token" });
  }
};

export const authorize =
  (perm: Permission) =>
  (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    if (!req.user) return res.status(401).json({ error: "Unauthenticated" });
    if (!hasPermission(req.user.role, perm)) {
      return res.status(403).json({ error: "Forbidden" });
    }
    next();
  };
Audit helper
src/middleware/audit.ts
ts
import { prisma } from "../db/prisma";
import { Role } from "@prisma/client";

export const audit = async (params: {
  userId: string;
  role: Role;
  action: string;
  entityType: string;
  entityId?: string;
  metadata?: any;
}) => {
  await prisma.auditLog.create({
    data: {
      userId: params.userId,
      role: params.role,
      action: params.action,
      entityType: params.entityType,
      entityId: params.entityId,
      metadata: params.metadata,
    },
  });
};
Trips routes
src/routes/trips.ts
ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { prisma } from "../db/prisma";
import { authenticate, authorize } from "../middleware/auth";
import { audit } from "../middleware/audit";

const router = Router();

router.get("/", authenticate, authorize("dashboard:view"), async (_req, res) => {
  const trips = await prisma.trip.findMany();
  res.json(trips);
});

router.post("/", authenticate, authorize("trip:create"), async (req, res) => {
  const trip = await prisma.trip.create({
    data: {
      id: `trip_${Date.now()}`,
      customerId: req.body.customerId,
      destination: req.body.destination,
      startDate: new Date(req.body.startDate),
      endDate: new Date(req.body.endDate),
      status: "CONFIRMED",
      totalPrice: req.body.totalPrice,
    },
  });

  await audit({
    userId: req.user!.id,
    role: req.user!.role,
    action: "trip:create",
    entityType: "trip",
    entityId: trip.id,
    metadata: trip,
  });

  res.status(201).json(trip);
});

router.put("/:id", authenticate, authorize("trip:update"), async (req, res) => {
  const trip = await prisma.trip.update({
    where: { id: req.params.id },
    data: {
      destination: req.body.destination,
      startDate: new Date(req.body.startDate),
      endDate: new Date(req.body.endDate),
      status: req.body.status,
      totalPrice: req.body.totalPrice,
    },
  });

  await audit({
    userId: req.user!.id,
    role: req.user!.role,
    action: "trip:update",
    entityType: "trip",
    entityId: trip.id,
    metadata: trip,
  });

  res.json(trip);
});

router.post("/:id/cancel", authenticate, authorize("trip:cancel"), async (req, res) => {
  const trip = await prisma.trip.update({
    where: { id: req.params.id },
    data: { status: "CANCELLED" },
  });

  await audit({
    userId: req.user!.id,
    role: req.user!.role,
    action: "trip:cancel",
    entityType: "trip",
    entityId: trip.id,
    metadata: trip,
  });

  res.json(trip);
});

export default router;
SCX routes (using previous ScxClient)
src/routes/scx.ts
ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authenticate, authorize } from "../middleware/auth";
import { scxClient } from "../scx/scxClient";
import { audit } from "../middleware/audit";

const router = Router();

router.post(
  "/quote",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:quote"),
  async (req, res) => {
    const quote = await scxClient.getQuote(req.body);

    await audit({
      userId: req.user!.id,
      role: req.user!.role,
      action: "scx:quote",
      entityType: "scx_quote",
      metadata: { request: req.body, response: quote },
    });

    res.json(quote);
  }
);

router.post(
  "/trade",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:trade"),
  async (req, res) => {
    const trade = await scxClient.executeTrade(req.body.quoteId);

    await audit({
      userId: req.user!.id,
      role: req.user!.role,
      action: "scx:trade",
      entityType: "scx_trade",
      entityId: trade.id,
      metadata: trade,
    });

    res.json(trade);
  }
);

router.get(
  "/positions",
  authenticate,
  authorize("scx:positions:view"),
  async (req, res) => {
    const positions = await scxClient.getPositions();

    await audit({
      userId: req.user!.id,
      role: req.user!.role,
      action: "scx:positions:view",
      entityType: "scx_positions",
      metadata: positions,
    });

    res.json(positions);
  }
);

export default router;
Audit API
src/routes/audit.ts
ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { authenticate, authorize } from "../middleware/auth";
import { prisma } from "../db/prisma";

const router = Router();

router.get("/", authenticate, authorize("audit:view"), async (req, res) => {
  const limit = Number(req.query.limit ?? 100);
  const logs = await prisma.auditLog.findMany({
    orderBy: { createdAt: "desc" },
    take: limit,
  });
  res.json(
    logs.map((l) => ({
      id: l.id,
      userId: l.userId,
      role: l.role,
      action: l.action,
      entityType: l.entityType,
      entityId: l.entityId,
      metadata: l.metadata,
      createdAt: l.createdAt,
    }))
  );
});

export default router;
Server entrypoint
src/index.ts
ts
import express from "express";
import cors from "cors";
import tripsRouter from "./routes/trips";
import scxRouter from "./routes/scx";
import auditRouter from "./routes/audit";

const app = express();
app.use(cors());
app.use(express.json());

app.get("/health", (_req, res) => res.json({ status: "ok" }));

app.use("/api/trips", tripsRouter);
app.use("/api/scx", scxRouter);
app.use("/api/audit", auditRouter);

const port = Number(process.env.PORT ?? 4000);
app.listen(port, () => console.log(`LuxuryOS API on ${port}`));
4. React console wiring (Dashboard + SCX + Audit)
You already have Dashboard and ScxPanel. Here’s the audit viewer tied to /api/audit.
console/src/api/audit.ts
ts
import { authFetch } from "./client";

export interface AuditLog {
  id: number;
  userId: string;
  role: string;
  action: string;
  entityType: string;
  entityId?: string;
  metadata?: any;
  createdAt: string;
}

export const fetchAuditLogs = (limit = 100): Promise<AuditLog[]> =>
  authFetch(`/api/audit?limit=${limit}`);
console/src/components/AuditLogViewer.tsx
tsx
import React, { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { fetchAuditLogs, AuditLog } from "../api/audit";
import { usePermissions } from "../hooks/usePermissions";

interface Props {
  role: "owner" | "admin" | "manager" | "agent" | "finance" | "viewer";
}

export const AuditLogViewer: React.FC<Props> = ({ role }) => {
  const { can } = usePermissions(role);
  const [logs, setLogs] = useState<AuditLog[]>([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!can("audit:view")) return;
    setLoading(true);
    fetchAuditLogs()
      .then(setLogs)
      .finally(() => setLoading(false));
  }, [can]);

  if (!can("audit:view")) {
    return <div>You do not have permission to view audit logs.</div>;
  }

  return (
    <div style={{ marginTop: 32 }}>
      <h2>Audit Log</h2>
      {loading ? (
        <p>Loading...</p>
      ) : (
        <table style={{ width: "100%", borderCollapse: "collapse", fontSize: 13 }}>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Time</th>
              <th>User</th>
              <th>Role</th>
              <th>Action</th>
              <th>Entity</th>
              <th>Metadata</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {logs.map((log) => (
              <tr key={log.id}>
                <td>{new Date(log.createdAt).toLocaleString()}</td>
                <td>{log.userId}</td>
                <td>{log.role}</td>
                <td>{log.action}</td>
                <td>
                  {log.entityType}
                  {log.entityId ? `:${log.entityId}` : ""}
                </td>
                <td>
                  <pre style={{ margin: 0, whiteSpace: "pre-wrap" }}>
                    {log.metadata ? JSON.stringify(log.metadata, null, 2) : "-"}
                  </pre>
                </td>
              </tr>
            ))}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      )}
    </div>
  );
};
console/src/App.tsx
tsx
import React from "react";

export const App: React.FC = () => (
  <div>
    <Dashboard role={role} />
    <ScxPanel role={role} />
    <AuditLogViewer role={role} />
  </div>
);
You now have a single, wired stack: Prisma schema + seed, Express API with RBAC and SCX integration, and a React console with dashboard, markets panel, and audit viewer. If you want, next step can be adding proper login/token issuance tied to the seeded users.
See my thinking
You’re basically asking: “If a real agency adopted DynastyOS, what exactly would they click, call, and run—and how does the code wire together?”
I’ll pick a concrete scenario and map it end‑to‑end, with flows → screens → APIs → code.
Scenario: County transportation + fuel + modernization
Agency: Grand Terrace County Transportation Agency Domains enabled: LogisticsOS, FuelOS, TreasuryOS, SCX, ModernizationOS, AnalyticsOS
1. Core flows
Flow A – Agency onboarding
Screens:
Admin Console → Agencies
Create agency: “Grand Terrace County Transportation”
Enable domains: Logistics, Fuel, Treasury, SCX, Modernization, Analytics
API calls:
POST /v1/agency/agencies
POST /v1/identity/tenants
POST /v1/identity/api-keys
Code (AgencyOS route):
ts
// services/agency-service/src/routes/agencies.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { createTenant, createApiKey } from "../../clients/IdentityClient";

export const agenciesRouter = Router();

agenciesRouter.post("/", async (req, res) => {
  const { name, type, region, domainsEnabled } = req.body;

  const agencyId = `AG-${Date.now()}`;

  const tenant = await createTenant({
    id: `TENANT-${agencyId}`,
    name,
    type: "AGENCY",
    agencyId
  });

  const apiKey = await createApiKey({
    tenantId: tenant.id,
    roles: ["ADMIN", "APP"],
    label: `${name}-console`
  });

  res.json({
    agency: {
      id: agencyId,
      name,
      type,
      region,
      domainsEnabled
    },
    tenant,
    apiKey
  });
});

Flow C – Run operations (shipments + fuel sales)
Screens:
Logistics → Shipments
Create shipments: “Bus parts from Depot to City Center”
Fuel → Sales
Record fuel transactions: “Fleet bus refuel”
API calls:
POST /v1/logistics/shipments
POST /v1/fuel/sales
Each call:
writes domain data (shipment, sale),
emits events to WorkflowOS and AnalyticsOS,
triggers TreasuryOS updates (internal credits/debits).
Code (Fuel sale → Treasury):
ts
// services/fuel-service/src/routes/sales.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { requireAgency } from "../middleware/agencyContext";
import { gasStationOS } from "../domain/GasStationOS";
import { treasuryClient } from "../../clients/TreasuryClient";
import { emitEvent } from "../../clients/EventBus";

export const salesRouter = Router();

salesRouter.post("/", requireAgency, async (req, res) => {
  const { agencyId } = req.context;
  const { siteId, liters, pricePerLiterUSD, payerTenantId } = req.body;

  const sale = gasStationOS.recordSale({
    agencyId,
    siteId,
    liters,
    pricePerLiterUSD,
    payerTenantId
  });

  const amount = liters * pricePerLiterUSD;

  await treasuryClient.credit({
    tenantId: payerTenantId,
    amount,
    currency: "USD",
    reference: `fuel_sale:${sale.id}`
  });

  await emitEvent("fuel.sale.recorded", {
    agencyId,
    saleId: sale.id,
    amount,
    payerTenantId
  });

  res.json(sale);
});
Flow D – Modernization project + financial instruments
Screens:
Modernization → Projects
Create project: “Upgrade Depot → City Center corridor”
Treasury → Instruments
Issue bond: “GT‑CORRIDOR‑BOND‑2026”
SCX → Markets
List bond as tradeable asset
API calls:
POST /v1/modernization/projects
POST /v1/treasury/instruments

Code (Instrument issuance):
ts
// services/treasury-service/src/routes/instruments.ts
import { Router } from "express";
import { instrumentService } from "../domain/InstrumentService";
import { emitEvent } from "../../clients/EventBus";

export const instrumentsRouter = Router();

instrumentsRouter.post("/", async (req, res) => {
  const { agencyId, type, terms, schedule } = req.body;

  const instrument = instrumentService.issue({
    agencyId,
    type,
    terms,
    schedule
  });

  await emitEvent("instrument.issued", {
    agencyId,
    instrumentId: instrument.id,
    type
  });

  res.json(instrument);
});
Flow E – Workflow automation
Examples:
“New corridor approval”:
Triggered by corridor.created.
Tasks:
legal review,
finance review,
operations sign‑off.
“Modernization project funding”:
Triggered by instrument.issued.
Tasks:
SCX listing,
Treasury disbursement schedule,
Analytics baseline snapshot.
API calls:
POST /v1/workflow/definitions
POST /v1/workflow/instances
POST /v1/workflow/tasks/:id/complete
Code (event → workflow):
ts
// services/workflow-service/src/EventHandler.ts
import { startWorkflow } from "./WorkflowEngine";

export async function handleEvent(event: { type: string; payload: any }) {
  switch (event.type) {
    case "corridor.created":
      await startWorkflow("corridor-approval", event.payload);
      break;
    case "instrument.issued":
      await startWorkflow("modernization-funding", event.payload);
      break;
  }
}

// services/analytics-service/src/routes/dashboards.ts

dashboardsRouter.get("/agency/:id", async (req, res) => {

Flow G – Mobile + partner portals
Mobile app:

view corridors,
log incidents,
check fuel sites.
Partner portals:

see fuel orders and inventory.

2. Screens overview (React console)
Agencies: create/manage agencies, domains enabled.
Tenants: per agency tenants, API keys.
Treasury: balances, instruments, settlements.
Logistics: corridors, shipments, carrier status.
Fuel: sites, inventory, sales.
Modernization: projects, milestones, funding instruments.
SCX Markets: assets, orders, trades.
Analytics: dashboards per agency and vertical.
Workflow: active workflows, tasks, approvals.
Each screen maps directly to /v1/... endpoints on the gateway.
3. Gateway + auth integration
Gateway routes:
/v1/agency/* → agency-service
/v1/identity/* → identity-service
/v1/treasury/* → treasury-service
/v1/logistics/* → logistics-service
/v1/fuel/* → fuel-service
/v1/modernization/* → modernization-service
/v1/scx/* → scx-service
/v1/analytics/* → analytics-service
/v1/workflow/* → workflow-service
Auth:
JWT for human users (agency staff, partners).
API keys for machine clients (carriers, printers, exchanges).
agencyId, tenantId, roles injected into request context.
4. What this gives a real agency
For a county transportation agency, DynastyOS now provides:
Operational backbone for corridors, fuel, logistics.
Financial rails for modernization funding and instruments.
Markets for trading those instruments (SCX).
Workflow automation for approvals and governance.
Analytics for performance and modernization impact.
Portals and mobile for staff and partners.
All of it is:
multi‑tenant,
multi‑agency,
CI/CD‑driven,
observable,
and wired into the same infrastructure you’ve already built.
If you want, next step can be: pick one flow (e.g., “corridor modernization bond issuance”) and I’ll write it as a fully detailed sequence diagram plus the exact request/response payloads.
See my thinking
Corridor modernization bond issuance – full sequence & payloads
We’ll walk one flow end‑to‑end:
Agency issues a corridor modernization bond, lists it on SCX, and wires it into TreasuryOS, WorkflowOS, and AnalyticsOS.
1. Sequence diagram (textual)
Actors:
Agency Console (React)
Gateway
AgencyOS
TreasuryOS
SCX
WorkflowOS
AnalyticsOS
Flow:
Agency admin opens Modernization → Projects and creates a corridor modernization project.
Console calls POST /v1/modernization/projects → Gateway → AgencyOS.
AgencyOS stores project, emits project.created.
Admin opens Treasury → Instruments and issues a bond for that project.
Console calls POST /v1/treasury/instruments → Gateway → TreasuryOS.
TreasuryOS stores instrument, emits instrument.issued.
WorkflowOS receives instrument.issued, starts workflow modernization-funding.
Workflow creates tasks: “SCX listing”, “Treasury schedule”, “Analytics baseline”.
SCX listing task runs (auto or manual):
Calls POST /v1/scx/assets to list bond as tradeable asset.
SCX stores asset, emits asset.listed.
Treasury schedule task runs:
Creates coupon/principal schedule in TreasuryOS.
Analytics baseline task runs:
AnalyticsOS snapshots current corridor metrics.
Result: bond is live on SCX, wired to TreasuryOS, tracked by WorkflowOS, visible in AnalyticsOS.

This is the full, step‑by‑step, payload‑by‑payload sequence for corridor modernization bond issuance—wired through ModernizationOS, TreasuryOS, SCX, WorkflowOS, AnalyticsOS, Gateway, and Console.
If you want, we can do the same level of detail for bond trading + settlement, or for luxury asset issuance and authentication.
